assistant: 2. If saving fails, the route returns an error without attempting email. The page remains locked and offers a retry.

Act as a financial-data researcher. This stage is research only: do not implement the landing page, write application code, or modify project files yet.

### 1. Verify the selection constraints first

Our current selection rule is:

- Three ETFs
- The same named benchmark index
- Comparable exposure to U.S. large-company equities
- Compliance with the assignment's NYSE listing requirement

Investigate what "traded on the NYSE" means in the context of the assignment. Do not silently treat NYSE and NYSE Arca as interchangeable.

Search for candidate ETFs using official exchange and issuer sources. Record the exact exchange venue for each candidate. If you cannot identify three funds satisfying both the same-benchmark rule and the listing requirement, stop and explain the constraint. Propose alternatives for my decision rather than weakening the criteria yourself.

### 2. Research candidate funds

For each candidate, verify:

- Legal fund name and ticker
- Issuer
- Exact exchange venue
- Benchmark index and investment objective
- Current stated annual expense ratio, distinguishing gross and net ratios where applicable
- Fund assets under management or net assets, if useful for the comparison
- Holdings count or another relevant diversification measure, if useful and available from a primary source
- Domicile and share-class details where relevant to the comparison

Keep the initial comparison focused. Do not collect financial metrics just because they are available; include only information that helps the target audience understand the differences between comparable funds.

### 3. Use primary sources

Prioritize:

- Official exchange listing information
- Current issuer product pages
- Current prospectuses and summary prospectuses
- Official issuer fact sheets or holdings files

Do not rely on search-result snippets, blogs, or third-party comparison websites as the sole evidence for fund facts.

For every data point, record:

- The exact value
- The source URL
- The date you checked the source
- The source's own data-as-of date, if provided
- A short note explaining what the source establishes

Clearly distinguish the date of checking a source from the date on which a financial value was measured.

### 4. Evaluate relevance to Czech retail investors

Investigate KID availability and any documented restrictions relevant to Czech retail investors separately from the exchange listing.

Do not assume that exchange listing proves a Czech retail investor can buy a fund. If broker-specific availability cannot be verified, state that limitation and do not make a purchase-availability claim.

### 5. Prepare the comparison and cost illustration

Propose a consistent comparison table for the three selected funds.

For the planned expense illustration, calculate approximate annual fund expenses on a hypothetical investment of 100,000 Kč using the stated expense ratio. Show the formula and assumptions. Explain that the estimate is not a complete measure of investor costs and excludes brokerage charges, bid-ask spreads, taxes, currency conversion, and other individual costs.

Do not include performance forecasts or present past performance as predictive of future returns.

### 6. Deliver your research findings

Present:

1. A shortlist of candidate funds and their exact listing venues.
2. A source-backed comparison table.
3. Source URLs and checking dates for every reported data point.
4. Unresolved questions or conflicts between sources.
5. A recommendation on whether the three-fund selection rule can be satisfied.
6. Any proposed change to the selection rule, clearly separated from the verified findings.

Keep the comparison educational, not a personalized investment recommendation.

Do not finalize the selection or proceed to implementation until I have reviewed the evidence and approved the funds.

For this step, I want research and product recommendations only. Do not create any files, PDFs, mockups, wireframe files, code, or other artifacts. Do not modify the repository.

## Our project context

- Audience: Czech-speaking beginner investors interested in U.S. equity ETFs.
- Provisional funds: STXF (broad U.S. large-cap exposure), STXG (growth-oriented equities), and STXV (value-oriented equities).
- The research and limitations are documented in `docs/etf-research.md`.
- Proposed lead magnet: a one-page comparison sheet explaining the three investment approaches, their benchmarks and methodologies, stated annual fund expenses, and relevant sources.
- Goal: build a conversion-focused, mobile-first landing page that collects leads in exchange for genuine value.
- Deadline: October 30, 2026.

## What I want you to do

### 1. Research similar landing pages

Find 5–7 real, relevant web pages. Prefer examples from Czech or European investment services, ETF comparison tools, financial education products, and successful-looking lead-magnet landing page structures.

For each reference, provide its name and exact URL, explain what the page offers, describe its structure, and identify useful patterns relevant to our project.

Examine:

- How the first screen communicates value.
- How the page previews its offering before asking for contact details.
- How financial comparisons are presented.
- What interactions help visitors understand the content.
- Where and how the email form is introduced.
- How the value exchange and next steps are explained.

Distinguish what you actually observed from your interpretation. Do not claim a page has high conversion unless reliable evidence supports that claim. If you cannot access a page or verify its form-submission flow, say so.

### 2. Critically evaluate the reference patterns

Do not simply summarize or copy the examples. Decide which approaches make sense for our specific audience, offer, and deadline.

Explain which patterns you recommend adopting, which you would reject, and why. Consider the fact that our product compares three different investment approaches rather than interchangeable funds.

### 3. Recommend our landing-page structure

Present your proposal directly in your response as a text-based outline, from top to bottom.

For each section, explain:

- What the visitor sees.
- What the section should communicate.
- What action the visitor can take.
- Why this section belongs at this point in the journey.

The structure must cover the full path:

Advertisement click → first screen → useful comparison preview → interaction → explanation of the lead magnet → short email form → successful submission → confirmation and delivery.

Also explain where source links, data dates, educational disclaimers, and privacy information should appear without overwhelming the first screen.

### 4. Make concrete product decisions

Based on your research, recommend:

- The best first-screen approach.
- The most useful preview before the form.
- Whether we should use expandable explanations, a comparison table, or another simple interaction.
- How to explain the value of the downloadable comparison sheet.
- The minimum necessary form fields.
- The simplest reliable delivery method to implement and test before the deadline.
- The appropriate order of the sections and the reasoning behind it.

Tell me what you believe is best for this project, not just a list of possible options. Explain meaningful trade-offs and uncertainties.

## Constraints

- Do not create or generate the actual PDF lead magnet.
- Do not create files or modify the repository.
- Do not write code or implement the landing page.
- Do not finalize the two advertising variants yet; those belong to Step 4.
- Do not invent conversion statistics or claim that a particular design is proven to convert better without supporting evidence.
- Keep the recommendations feasible within the remaining time.
- Present your research, links, analysis, and recommendations as text in your response.

Finish with your recommended landing-page structure and the decisions that need my approval. Wait for my approval before implementation.

Document the following decisions:

## 1. Visitor journey

- Visitor arrives from an advertisement.
- The first screen immediately matches the advertisement's promise.
- The visitor sees a useful preview comparing STXF, STXG, and STXV.
- Expandable explanations help clarify broad U.S. large-cap, growth, value, and expense-ratio terminology.
- The page explains what additional information is included in the full comparison.
- The visitor submits the required first name and email.
- A separate optional, initially unchecked checkbox records consent for future marketing emails.

## 2. Lead capture and unlocking

- The backend records the lead before the full comparison is unlocked.
- Repeated submissions with the same normalized email should not create duplicate lead records.
- After successful recording, the full comparison unlocks on the same page.
- If recording fails, the comparison remains locked and the visitor can retry.
- No account system, one-time access tokens, or PDF generation is part of the MVP.

## 3. Transactional email

- Send a personalized confirmation email using the visitor's first name.
- Include a link to the landing page so the visitor can return later and enter the same email to access the comparison again.
- If email sending fails after the lead is saved, keep the comparison unlocked and show a truthful, non-blocking message.
- Never claim an email was sent unless the sending attempt was successful.

## 4. Privacy and marketing

- Explain why the name and email are collected.
- Keep marketing consent separate from access to the comparison.
- Do not send marketing emails without the required consent or other verified legal basis.
- Record any unresolved privacy and provider-configuration requirements for checking before launch.

## 5. Product and delivery constraints

- This is a single-page educational landing site, not a full web application.
- The comparison is not personalized investment advice.
- The comparison contains public financial information; the email gate is a lead-generation mechanism, not security.
- Use free hosting and free service tiers only.
- Do not promise features that are not implemented and tested.

Use clear Markdown headings and concise wording. Distinguish approved decisions from implementation details that remain to be confirmed.

Update the existing ETF comparison landing page for Task B. Preserve the current visual identity, the working mobile layout, and the existing Python/FastAPI/Jinja2/HTML/CSS/Vanilla JavaScript/SQLAlchemy/PostgreSQL stack.

- `docs/etf-research.md`

- `docs/ads.md`
- The current templates, CSS, JavaScript, lead route, database logic, email service, and tests.

## 1. Expand the ETF comparison responsibly

Target a full comparison of 10 ETFs, with 3 representative ETFs visible before the lead form and the full table available after a successful lead submission.

Before adding new ETFs:

- Follow the already documented definition of an eligible NYSE listing. Do not silently broaden the exchange scope.
- Verify every additional ETF using official issuer/fund documentation and reliable exchange or regulatory sources.
- Record the ticker, fund name, issuer, investment approach, benchmark/index, expense ratio, source URLs, and verification date in `docs/etf-research.md`.
- Prefer a selection that covers the intended investment approaches and includes more than one issuer.
- Do not invent facts, fees, listings, benchmarks, or source links.
- If 10 eligible funds cannot be verified within the existing scope, report the limitation and recommend the largest defensible set. Data accuracy is more important than hitting exactly 10.

Keep the table readable on desktop and mobile. Do not add complex filters, sorting, or a new frontend framework. Do not present the funds as a universal best-to-worst ranking.

## 2. Preserve the lead and email behavior

Keep the existing name/email form and optional marketing consent.

The full comparison must unlock immediately after the lead has been successfully saved to the database. It must remain unlocked if the subsequent confirmation-email attempt fails.

Email status must remain truthful:

- A successful provider response may be reported as accepted by the provider, not guaranteed delivered.
- An unconfigured or failed email must not undo the saved lead or relock the comparison.

Use clear Czech CTA copy such as:

- `Odemknout úplné srovnání`
- `Zadejte e-mail a odemkněte úplné srovnání`

Do not imply mandatory newsletter subscription. Keep marketing consent separate, optional, and unchecked by default.

## 3. Add one restrained promotional card

Add a small, non-blocking slide-in card that appears about 8 seconds after page load and invites the visitor to open the full comparison.

Requirements:

- Do not show it immediately on page load.
- The card must be dismissible and must not obscure the whole page.
- Show it at most once per browser tab/session, and do not show it again after the visitor dismisses it or unlocks the comparison.
- Its CTA should scroll to or focus the existing lead form; do not create a second, duplicate form.
- On mobile, keep it compact and ensure it does not cover important content or controls.
- Use accessible markup and keyboard focus behavior where needed.
- Do not add third-party popup libraries.

## 4. Add subtle animations

Add no more than two or three restrained micro-interactions, such as:

- A short entrance animation for the hero content.
- A fade/slide animation for the promotional card.
- A subtle reveal when the full comparison is unlocked.

Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. Avoid looping animations, exaggerated movement, and layout shifts.

## 5. Funnel measurement

Inspect the current implementation first. If funnel measurement is missing, add the smallest feasible privacy-conscious solution using the existing stack. Cover page view, card shown/dismissed, CTA interaction, form submission success, comparison unlocked, and email accepted/failed. Do not send names or email addresses in analytics events. Do not add external analytics services, a dashboard, or a new paid dependency. Document how the funnel events can be inspected locally.

## 6. Documentation and verification

Update the relevant research and conversion-journey documents to match the actual implementation.

Add or update tests for the lead-save/unlock behavior and email-failure handling. Do not launch the application, open a browser, perform GUI testing, or deploy; I will verify those manually.

Do not build login, PDF generation, user accounts, one-time tokens, advanced analytics, or unrelated features. Do not finalize deployment instructions until the local implementation has been reviewed.

When finished, report:

1. Files changed.
2. Which ETFs were verified and which sources support them.
3. Exactly how the promotional card behaves.
4. What funnel events are recorded and where to inspect them.
5. Tests added or updated and any remaining limitations.

    def submit_lead(

        return {

        }

    return application

STXK

# Strive Small-Cap ETF

Fund Objective
The Strive Small-Cap ETF (STXK) is a passively managed exchange traded fund (ETF) that seeks exposure to the smallest 600 corporations by market capitalization of the Bloomberg US 600 Total Return Index.

Fund Description
STXK offers exposure to small-capitalization U.S. corporations through a cost-efficient index product. Through Corporate Governance practices, including voting proxy shares and proactively engaging with management teams and boards, Strive<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="4394" />high relative to recent years which boosted net interest income for regional banks, while insurance stocks also gained. The energy sector was also a notable detractor amid the expected production increase amid slowing energy demand in major economies. Both the energy and materials sectors struggled with trade uncertainty and the impacts of tariffs. The health care sector also detracted broadly from performance.

(() => {
  const form = document.querySelector("#lead-form");
  if (!form) return;

  const submitButton = form.querySelector(".form-submit");
  const formStatus = document.querySelector("#form-status");
  const fullComparison = document.querySelector("#full-comparison");
  const unlockSuccess = document.querySelector("#unlock-success");
  const visitorName = document.querySelector("#visitor-name");
  const emailResult = document.querySelector("#email-result");
  const firstName = document.querySelector("#first-name");
  const email = document.querySelector("#email");

  function clearErrors() {
    document.querySelectorAll(".field-error").forEach((node) => { node.textContent = ""; });
    document.querySelectorAll(".field-group input").forEach((node) => node.removeAttribute("aria-invalid"));
  }

  function showValidationErrors(details) {
    const messages = {
      first_name: "Zadejte prosím své jméno.",
      email: "Zadejte prosím platnou e-mailovou adresu.",
    };
    let shown = false;
    for (const issue of details || []) {
      const field = issue.loc?.at(-1);
      const message = messages[field];
      const input = field === "first_name" ? firstName : field === "email" ? email : null;
      const error = field === "first_name"
        ? document.querySelector("#first-name-error")
        : field === "email" ? document.querySelector("#email-error") : null;
      if (input && error && message) {
        input.setAttribute("aria-invalid", "true");
        error.textContent = message;
        if (!shown) input.focus();
        shown = true;
      }
    }
    if (!shown) {
      formStatus.textContent = "Zkontrolujte prosím jméno a e-mailovou adresu.";
    }
  }

  form.addEventListener("submit", async (event) => {
    event.preventDefault();
    clearErrors();
    formStatus.classList.remove("is-success");
    formStatus.textContent = "";
    emailResult.textContent = "";

def make_engine(database_url: [REDACTED]
    options = {"pool_pre_ping": True}
    if database_url.startswith("sqlite"):
        options["connect_args"] = {"check_same_thread": False}
        if database_url in {"sqlite://", "sqlite:///:memory:"}:
            options["poolclass"] = StaticPool
    return create_engine(database_url, **options)


def make_session_factory(engine):
    return sessionmaker(bind=engine, autoflush=False, expire_on_commit=False)


def get_session(session_factory) -> Generator[Session, None, None]:
    session = session_factory()
    try:
        yield session
    finally:
        session.close()


def record_lead(
    session: Session,
    *,
    first_name: str,
    email: str,
    marketing_consent: bool,
) -> None:
    """Insert or update a lead under its normalized, unique email address."""
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    consent_at = now if marketing_consent else None
    values = {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "email": email,
        "marketing_consent": marketing_consent,
        "marketing_consent_at": consent_at,
        "created_at": now,
        "updated_at": now,
    }

    dialect = session.get_bind().dialect.name
    if dialect == "postgresql":
        insert = postgresql_insert(Lead)
    elif dialect == "sqlite":
        insert = sqlite_insert(Lead)
    else:
        raise RuntimeError(f"Unsupported database dialect: {dialect}")

    statement = insert.values(**values).on_conflict_do_update(
        index_elements=[Lead.email],
        set_={
            "first_name": insert.excluded.first_name,
            "marketing_consent": insert.excluded.marketing_consent,
            "marketing_consent_at": insert.excluded.marketing_consent_at,
            "updated_at": insert.excluded.updated_at,
        },
    )
    session.execute(statement)
    session.commit()

def test_database_failure_does_not_unlock_or_attempt_email(monkeypatch):
    called = []

    def sender(*args):
        called.append(args)
        return EmailResult("accepted")

    _, client = make_client(sender)
    monkeypatch.setattr(main_module, "record_lead", lambda *_args, **_kwargs: (_ for _ in ()).throw(RuntimeError()))
    with client:
        response = client.post(
            "/api/leads",
            json={"first_name": "Jana", "email": "[REDACTED_EMAIL]", "marketing_consent": False},
        )

    assert response.status_code == 503
    assert response.json()["detail"] == "Údaje se nepodařilo uložit. Zkuste to prosím znovu."
    assert called == []


def test_unconfigured_email_is_reported_without_claiming_delivery():
    _, client = make_client()
    with client:
        response = client.post(
            "/api/leads",
            json={"first_name": "Eva", "email": "[REDACTED_EMAIL]", "marketing_consent": False},
        )

    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert response.json()["email_status"] == "not_configured"


def test_real_email_adapter_does_not_fake_sending_without_credentials(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.delenv("RESEND_API_KEY", raising=False)
    monkeypatch.delenv("RESEND_FROM_EMAIL", raising=False)

    result = send_confirmation("Eva", "[REDACTED_EMAIL]")

    assert result.status == "not_configured"

# ETF research: provisional comparison set

## Research scope and methodology

Research was checked on **October 28, 2026**. The assignment's NYSE requirement is interpreted strictly as listing on the **New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) venue itself**; NYSE Arca is excluded. Issuer product pages, current prospectuses and supplements, and SEC filings were used as primary sources. The venue was checked against SEC N-CEN exchange records, which identify the exchange as “NEW YORK STOCK EXCHANGE, INC.” and corroborated against the issuer pages' primary-exchange fields. No separate NYSE listing-page URL was preserved in the completed research record. Each available source URL and check date is recorded below.

This is an educational comparison of three provisional candidates, not an investment recommendation. No performance forecast is included. Where a source reports a financial value's own “as of” date, that date is distinguished from the date the source was checked.

## Provisional candidates

All three funds are series of **EA Series Trust**. Available issuer disclosures identify **Empowered Funds, LLC, doing business as ETF Architect**, as investment adviser and **Strive Asset Management, LLC** as sub-adviser. The issuer's disclosures also identify **PINE Distributors LLC** as distributor. These details were checked October 28, 2026 in the linked issuer materials and fund filings. The funds remain provisional pending review of this record.

|---|---|---|---|---:|---|

| STXF ticker-change supplement | https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1592900/000159290026002347/strivestrv-stxf497etickerc.htm | Effective 2026-06-08; checked 2026-10-28 | Establishes STRV-to-STXF ticker change, not a benchmark change. |
| September 2026 fund supplement | https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1592900/000159290026004237/strive497pmchange.htm | Dated 2026-09-15; checked 2026-10-28 | Supplements the Oct. 31, 2025 summary prospectuses with portfolio-manager information; no change to the researched benchmark or expense fields. |
| EU PRIIPs Regulation (Regulation (EU) No 1286/2014) | https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/?uri=celex%3A32014R1286 | Checked 2026-10-28 | Establishes the EU legal framework for key information documents for packaged retail and insurance-based investment products, including producer and distributor duties. It does not establish whether a Czech broker offers any of these three funds. |
| Czech National Bank PRIIPs KID guidance | https://www.cnb.cz/en/supervision-financial-market/legislation/key-information-documents-priips/index.html | Checked 2026-10-28 | Summarizes the general KID requirement: the product producer provides a KID for retail-targeted investment products, and those selling or advising provide it to an investor before an agreement. It does not confirm a KID exists for STXF, STXG or STXV, or that any broker makes them available. |
| Czech National Bank PRIIPs KID Q&A | https://www.cnb.cz/en/faq/Questions-and-answers-on-the-PRIIPs-KID | Checked 2026-10-28 | Provides CNB responses on practical KID issues, including language, drawing up/publication/provision, execution-only services, and investor access to the relevant KID. It does not establish broker-specific availability of STXF, STXG or STXV to Czech retail investors. |

# Conversion Journey

## Approved visitor journey

1. The visitor arrives from an advertisement. The first screen immediately reflects the advertisement's promise and previews the comparison of STXF, STXG, and STXV.
2. The visitor can expand short explanations of broad U.S. large-cap, growth, value, and expense-ratio terminology.
3. The page explains what additional verified information and caveats are included in the full comparison.
4. The visitor enters a required first name and email address. A separate, optional marketing-consent checkbox is initially unchecked. Access to the comparison does not depend on marketing consent.
5. The backend records the lead before the full comparison unlocks on the same page. Repeated submissions with the same normalized email must not create duplicate lead records.
6. After successful recording, the full comparison unlocks. If recording fails, it remains locked and the visitor can retry.
7. The service attempts to send a personalized transactional confirmation email using the visitor's first name. It links to the landing page, where the visitor can enter the same email to unlock the comparison again.
8. If email sending fails after the lead is recorded, the comparison remains unlocked and the page shows a truthful, non-blocking message. The page must not claim that an email was sent unless the sending attempt succeeded.

The full comparison is on the landing page. PDF generation, accounts, and one-time access tokens are outside the MVP.

## Lead capture and privacy

The name and email are collected to record the request for the comparison and, for the email address, to send the requested transactional confirmation. The marketing checkbox records a separate choice; it is not required to access the comparison. Marketing emails must not be sent without the required consent or another verified legal basis.

Before launch, confirm the privacy notice, lawful basis an<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="2648" />rrent issuer page. Preserve this caveat in the full comparison or source note:

`Aktuální stránka emitenta označuje benchmark fondu STXF jako Bloomberg US Large Cap Index. Prospekt z října 2025 používá název Bloomberg 500 Index a zpráva za červen 2026 obsahuje v části věnované výkonnosti také nekonzistentní označení. Emitent tento rozdíl formálně nevysvětlil. Tato označení zde nepovažujeme za oficiálně zaměnitelná.`

The ticker changed from STRV to STXF effective June 8, 2026; this was a ticker change, not a benchmark change. The research was checked on **October 28, 2026** and financial data should be rechecked before publication.

## Initial concept recommendation

| Criterion | Concept A: understanding approaches | Concept B: fund characteristics |
|---|---|---|
| Message clarity | Strong for a beginner; starts with familiar questions | Specific, but “benchmark methodology” and expenses may feel technical |
| Audience relevance | Fits someone learning the investment-style labels | Fits someone already comparing fund facts |
| Credibility | Supported by simple explanations and visible fund facts | Strong when paired with sources and caveats |
| Product alignment | Directly reflects the three different approaches | Closely reflects the comparison fields and verified facts |
| Risk of misunderstanding | Could be mistaken for strategy advice unless differences are explicit | Could be mistaken for a ranking by fees unless limits are prominent |

**Recommended initial direction: Concept A.** It introduces the difference visitors need to understand before asking them to interpret index methodologies and expenses. Keep the preview concrete—with fund names, approaches, and stated expense ratios—so it delivers value before the form. Concept B remains a distinct alternative for visitors already researching fund characteristics. This is a strategic judgment; neither concept has demonstrated conversion performance.

On mobile it remains a small fixed card rather than a full-screen overlay.\n+\n+Application funnel events are sent to the same FastAPI service and written as structured event-name/placement log lines. They are not interchangeable or ranked; the comparison is educational, not personalized investment advice.\n+- The set contains seven Strive funds and three Baron funds. It is a small two-issuer selection, not a representative view of the ETF market. STXF and STXK have source naming caveats recorded in `docs/etf-research.md`.\n+- The 10-row comparison remains horizontally scrollable on narrow screens; the first column stays available as a ticker/name reference.\n*** End Patch";

"""FastAPI application for the single-page ETF comparison."""

from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
import logging
import os
from pathlib import Path

from dotenv import load_dotenv
from fastapi import Depends, FastAPI, HTTPException, Request
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse, Response
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, EmailStr, Field, field_validator
from sqlalchemy.exc import SQLAlchemyError
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
from starlette.templating import Jinja2Templates

from app.database import Base, get_session, make_engine, make_session_factory, record_lead
from app.email_service import EmailResult, send_confirmation
from app.funds import (
    CHECKED_ON,
    COMMON_ISSUER_NOTE,
    EXCHANGE_SOURCE,
    FUNDS,
    PREVIEW_FUNDS,
    STXF_HISTORY_URL,
    STXF_TICKER_URL,
    STXG_SUPPLEMENT_URL,
)

load_dotenv()

logger = logging.getLogger("etf_comparison")
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
templates = Jinja2Templates(directory=str(ROOT / "templates"))


class LeadSubmission(BaseModel):
    first_name: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=80)
    email: EmailStr
    marketing_consent: bool = False

    @field_validator("first_name", mode="before")
    @classmethod
    def trim_first_name(cls, value):
        if not isinstance(value, str):
        logger.info("funnel_event=%s placement=%s", event.event, event.placement or "none")
        return Response(status_code=204)

    @application.post("/api/leads")
    def submit_lead(
        submission: LeadSubmission,
        session: Session = Depends(session_dependency),
    ):
        normalized_email = str(submission.email).strip().casefold()
        try:
            record_lead(
                session,
                first_name=submission.first_name,
                email=normalized_email,
                marketing_consent=submission.marketing_consent,
            )
        except (SQLAlchemyError, RuntimeError):
            session.rollback()
            logger.error("Lead recording failed; comparison remains locked.")
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=503,
                detail="Údaje se nepodařilo uložit. Zkuste to prosím znovu.",
            ) from None

        try:
            email_result: EmailResult = email_sender(submission.first_name, normalized_email)
            email_status = email_result.status
        except Exception:
            # The lead is already committed; email failure must not relock the page.
            logger.error("Confirmation email attempt failed after lead recording.")
            email_status = "failed"

        if email_status not in {"accepted", "failed", "not_configured"}:
            email_status = "failed"

        return {
            "recorded": True,
            "email_status": email_status,
            "message": "Děkujeme, vaše srovnání je nyní odemčeno.",
        }

    return application


app = create_app()

# Local Development

This project is a FastAPI and Jinja2 landing page with PostgreSQL lead storage. It does not deploy or create cloud resources. The form sends email only when Resend credentials and a verified sender are configured; otherwise it reports that no email was sent.

## Requirements

- Python 3.11 or later.
- PostgreSQL 14 or later, with `initdb`, `pg_ctl`, `createdb`, and `psql` available in the shell. On macOS, install PostgreSQL with Homebrew if needed.

## Create a local PostgreSQL database

From the project root, initialize a local-only database cluster. These example commands use port `55432` so an existing PostgreSQL service on port `5432` is not disturbed. The `trust` authentication options are for this disposable local development cluster only.

```sh
mkdir -p .local/postgres
initdb -D .local/postgres/data -U postgres --auth-local=trust --auth-host=trust
pg_ctl -D .local/postgres/data -l .local/postgres/server.log -o "-h 127.0.0.1 -p 55432" start
createdb -h 127.0.0.1 -p 55432 -U postgres etf_comparison
```

If you already have a database, skip this section and set its connection URL in `.env`.

## Install and run

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
cp .env.example .env
```

The example database URL matches the local cluster above. Start the application:

```sh
uvicorn app.main:app --reload
```

Open `http://127.0.0.1:8000` in your browser. The first run creates the `leads` table automatically. Stop the local PostgreSQL cluster when finished:

```sh
pg_ctl -D .local/postgres/data stop
```

## Email configuration

Leave `RESEND_API_KEY` and `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` blank to verify the lead-saving and comparison-unlocking flow without sending email. The page will state that confirmation email was not configured. To test real sending, configure a Resend key and verified sender in your private `.env`; never commit that file. <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="351" />son unlocks again.
7. Temporarily stop PostgreSQL and submit a lead. Confirm the page stays locked, provides a retry message, and does not claim a lead was recorded.
8. Review the full comparison's source links, STXF naming caveat, expense assumptions, data date, privacy placeholders, and educational disclaimer.

## Before public launch

Supply and review the actual data controller's identity and contact information, lawful basis and consent wording, data retention and deletion process, provider/data-transfer disclosures, sender domain and email configuration, and final privacy information. Verify the current limitations and terms of any free hosting or database services before configuring them. None are configured by this local-only step.

# Local Development

This project is a FastAPI and Jinja2 landing page with PostgreSQL lead storage. It does not deploy or create cloud resources. The form sends email only when Resend credentials and a verified sender are configured; otherwise it reports that no email was sent.

## Requirements

- Python 3.11 or later.
- PostgreSQL 14 or later, with `initdb`, `pg_ctl`, `createdb`, and `psql` available in the shell. On macOS, install PostgreSQL with Homebrew if needed.

## Create a local PostgreSQL database

From the project root, initialize a local-only database cluster. These example commands use port `55432` so an existing PostgreSQL service on port `5432` is not disturbed. The `trust` authentication options are for this disposable local development cluster only.

```sh
mkdir -p .local/postgres
initdb -D .local/postgres/data -U postgres --auth-local=trust --auth-host=trust
pg_ctl -D .local/postgres/data -l .local/postgres/server.log -o "-h 127.0.0.1 -p 55432" start
createdb -h 127.0.0.1 -p 55432 -U postgres etf_comparison
```

If you already have a database, skip this section and set its connection URL in `.env`.

## Install and run

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
cp .env.example .env
```

The example database URL matches the local cluster above. Start the application:

```sh
uvicorn app.main:app --reload
```

Open `http://127.0.0.1:8000` in your browser. The first run creates the `leads` table automatically. Stop the local PostgreSQL cluster when finished:

```sh
pg_ctl -D .local/postgres/data stop
```

## Email configuration

Leave `RESEND_API_KEY` and `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` blank to verify the lead-saving and comparison-unlocking flow without sending email. The page will state that confirmation email was not configured. To test real sending, configure a Resend key and verified sender in your private `.env`; never commit that file. `<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="44" />ted in-memory SQLite database to avoid requiring the local PostgreSQL service. They do not replace manually checking the application against PostgreSQL.

When the same normalized email is submitted again, the row is updated with the latest name and checkbox state. An unchecked box clears the stored marketing-consent flag and timestamp; marketing sending is not implemented.

## Manual browser review

1. At a narrow mobile width, confirm the heading, benefit, and preview CTA are visible without scrolling.
2. Check the three preview cards on mobile and the compact comparison table on desktop.
3. Expand each glossary item with mouse, touch, and keyboard; confirm the focus indicator is visible and reduced-motion preference is respected.
4. Submit an empty form and an invalid email; confirm the browser and server reject them with clear messages.
5. With a working local PostgreSQL connection and email variables blank, submit a valid lead. Confirm the full comparison unlocks only after the response, the email-not-configured message is truthful, and the lead appears once in PostgreSQL.
6. Submit the same email again with different casing and surrounding spaces. Confirm there is still only one lead record and the comparison unlocks again.
7. Temporarily stop PostgreSQL and submit a lead. Confirm the page stays locked, provides a retry message, and does not claim a lead was recorded.
8. Review the full comparison's source links, STXF naming caveat, expense assumptions, data date, privacy placeholders, and educational disclaimer.

## Before public launch

Supply and review the actual data controller's identity and contact information, lawful basis and consent wording, data retention and deletion process, provider/data-transfer disclosures, sender domain and email configuration, and final privacy information. Verify the current limitations and terms of any free hosting or database services before configuring them. None are configured by this local-only step.

    @application.post("/api/confirmation-email")
    def send_confirmation_email(
        submission: ConfirmationRequest,
        session: Session = Depends(session_dependency),
    ):
        normalized_email = str(submission.email).strip().casefold()
        lead = session.scalar(select(Lead).where(Lead.email == normalized_email))
        if lead is None:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Uloženou žádost se nepodařilo najít.")

        try:
            result: EmailResult = email_sender(lead.first_name, normalized_email)
            email_status = result.status
        except Exception:
            logger.error("Confirmation email attempt failed after lead recording.")
            email_status = "failed"

        if email_status not in {"accepted", "failed", "not_configured"}:
            email_status = "failed"
        return {"email_status": email_status}

    return application

docs/ads.md:3:This document records the approved Step 4 advertising concepts and Czech-language copy, aligned with the implemented local MVP: visitors preview three funds; the full on-page comparison covers ten funds. The concepts remain hypotheses; no conversion performance is claimed.
docs/ads.md:5:## Advertising concept A — Understanding the three approaches
docs/ads.md:11:**Marketing angle:** Explain three distinct approaches in plain Czech and provide a useful comparison preview.
docs/ads.md:13:**Promise:** See how broad U.S. large-cap exposure, growth-oriented equities, and value-oriented equities differ in a three-fund preview; the full page comparison covers ten NYSE-listed U.S. equity ETFs with distinct approaches.
docs/ads.md:18:- **Primary text:** Co znamená široké zaměření na velké americké firmy, růstový styl a hodnotový styl? Prohlédněte si ná<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1582" />t, and the CNB Q&A explains practical topics such as language, publication/provision, and execution-only services. These sources do **not** establish broker-specific availability of STXF, STXG, or STXV to Czech retail investors. Fund-level KID availability and broker access remain unverified.
docs/etf-research.md:123:| SEC N-CEN exchange listing record | https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1592900/000119312526388859/xslFormN-CEN_X05/primary_doc.xml | Filed 2026; checked 2026-10-28 | Identifies the exchange as “NEW YORK STOCK EXCHANGE, INC.” for STXF, STXG and STXV. The record does not establish Czech broker availability. |
docs/etf-research.md:127:| EU PRIIPs Regulation (Regulation (EU) No 1286/2014) | https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/?uri=celex%3A32014R1286 | Checked 2026-10-28 | Establishes the EU legal framework for key information documents for packaged retail and insurance-based investment products, including producer and distributor duties. It does not establish whether a Czech broker offers any of these three funds. |
docs/etf-research.md:128:| Czech National Bank PRIIPs KID guidance | https://www.cnb.cz/en/supervision-financial-market/legislation/key-information-documents-priips/index.html | Checked 2026-10-28 | Summarizes the general KID requirement: the product producer provides a KID for retail-targeted investment products, and those selling or advising provide it to an investor before an agreement. It does not confirm a KID exists for STXF, STXG or STXV, or that any broker makes them available. |
docs/etf-research.md:129:| Czech National Bank PRIIPs KID Q&A | https://www.cnb.cz/en/faq/Questions-and-answers-on-the-PRIIPs-KID | Checked 2026-10-28 | Provides CNB responses on practical KID issues, including language, drawing up/publication/provision, execution-only services, and investor access to the relevant KID. It does not establish broker-specific availability of STXF, STXG or STXV to Czech retail investors. |
